<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every fixed integer <i>r</i> ≥ 4, we prove that every <i>K</i><sub><i>r</i></sub>-free graph of sufficiently large maximum degree Δ has correspondence chromatic number $`O_r(\Delta/\log\Delta)`$. This resolves the Alon–Krivelevich–Sudakov coloring conjecture in the stronger correspondence-coloring form. The same bound, with a constant depending on <i>F</i>, holds when any fixed graph <i>F</i> is excluded as an ordinary subgraph. Ordinary and list coloring satisfy the same bounds.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $r\ge4$ there are constants $C_r>0$ and $\Delta_r\ge3$ such that every $K_r$-free graph $G$ of maximum degree $\Delta\ge\Delta_r$ satisfies $$\chi_{\mathrm{DP}}(G) \le \left\lceil C_r\frac{\Delta}{\log\Delta}\right\rceil.$$ The constants depend only on $r$.
  - Explanation: The correspondence chromatic number admits a logarithmic improvement over the maximum-degree bound when a fixed clique is excluded.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-forbidden)): For every finite graph $F$ there are constants $C_F>0$ and $\Delta_F\ge3$ such that every $F$-free graph $G$ of maximum degree $\Delta\ge\Delta_F$ satisfies $$\chi_{\mathrm{DP}}(G) \le \left\lceil C_F\frac{\Delta}{\log\Delta}\right\rceil.$$
  - Explanation: Excluding any fixed finite graph as an ordinary subgraph gives the same order of correspondence-coloring bound.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-adjustment)): For every integer $r\ge4$ there are constants $A=A(r)>0$, $B=B(r)\ge1$, and $\Lambda_r\ge1$ with the following property. Let $\Lambda\ge\Lambda_r$, and let $H$ be a finite $K_r$-free graph whose vertex set has a partition $\mathcal D$ such that each vertex has at most one neighbor in each part. For any positive vertex weights with $s_i\le\Lambda$, there is a finite family of the multiplier and drop operations defined above, with vectors $g^{(j)}$, listed supports $J_j$, and rates $a_j\ge0$, such that $$\label{eq:adjustment} t_i+\sum_j a_jg_i^{(j)}\le B d_i, \qquad \sum_{j:\,i\in J_j}a_j\le\Lambda^4 \qquad(i\in V(H)).$$ The constants depend only on $r$.
  - Explanation: Local weight operations offset triangle contributions at every vertex while bounding participation in each partition part and cumulative rates.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse-multipliers)): For every integer $r\ge2$ there is a constant $D_r\ge1$ such that the following holds. Let $F$ be a finite $K_r$-free graph with positive vertex weights $q_i$ of total mass $s$. For every $0<\epsilon\le1/2$, there are jointly distributed random multipliers $n_i$ satisfying $$\label{eq:sparse-multipliers} 0\le n_i\le D_r\epsilon^{-(r-2)},\qquad \mathbb En_i=1,\qquad \mathbb E\sum_{iu\in E(F)}q_iq_u n_i n_u\le\epsilon s^2,$$ where the edge sum is unordered.
  - Explanation: Mean-one random multipliers reduce expected weighted edge mass while keeping every multiplier bounded.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-cross)): Assume the prices satisfy <a href="#eq:dual-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dual-test">[eq:dual-test]</a>, and fix a level $b$. Let $S,D\subseteq V(H_b)$, where $S\cup D$ is contained in a ball of radius $R_0$ in $H$ and meets every part of $\mathcal D$ in at most $P_0$ vertices. Suppose that $$32\le h\le20\ell_0^2,\qquad q(S),q(D)\le e^h, \qquad 0\le\eta\le2,$$ and that $\alpha_i,\beta_i\le\eta$ for $i\in S$. If $A$ in <a href="#eq:adjustment-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:adjustment-parameters">[eq:adjustment-parameters]</a> is sufficiently large depending only on $r$, then $$\label{eq:local-cross} e_{q,H_b}(S,D)\le C_1\bigl(\eta hq(S)+e^{-3h}\bigr),$$ where $C_1=C_1(r)$.
  - Explanation: Dual prices control the directed weighted edge mass between local sets subject to radius, participation, mass, and coefficient conditions.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-cross-transfer)): Let $F$ be a graph with a homomorphism $p:F\to H_b$ that is injective on directed edges. Give every vertex of $F$ the weight, prices, coefficients, and part of its image. Then every vertex of $F$ has at most one neighbor in each inherited part. Moreover, <a href="#eq:local-cross" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-cross">[eq:local-cross]</a> holds in $F$ for any $S,D\subseteq V(F)$ satisfying the mass and coefficient hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:local-cross" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:local-cross">5</a>, provided $S\cup D$ lies in a ball of radius $R_0$ in $F$ and meets each inherited part in at most $P_0$ vertices.
  - Explanation: The local cross estimate and the neighbor-per-part property survive a directed-edge-injective projection.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-triangle-bound)): For the graphs $H_b$, weights $q$, and prices constructed above from <a href="#eq:dual-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dual-test">[eq:dual-test]</a>, with $A$ chosen as in Lemma <a href="#lem:local-cross" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:local-cross">5</a>, the quantities in <a href="#eq:price-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:price-mass">[eq:price-mass]</a> and <a href="#eq:dyadic-triangle-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dyadic-triangle-mass">[eq:dyadic-triangle-mass]</a> satisfy $$T_0\le O_r(M_x).$$ The implicit constant depends only on $r$.
  - Explanation: The total dyadic triangle mass is bounded by the priced edge mass, uniformly in the number of levels.
- **C8** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-triangle-splitting)): Let $32\le\ell\le\ell_0$, and let $F$ be a nonempty finite graph with no isolated vertices, with a homomorphism into some $H_b$ injective on directed edges. Give its vertices the inherited weights, prices, and parts, and suppose that <a href="#eq:prepared-level" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prepared-level">[eq:prepared-level]</a> holds. There is a graph $F'$ obtained by splitting vertices and retaining at most one copy of each edge of $F$, with unchanged vertex weights, such that every neighborhood of $F'$ has weight at most $e^{\sqrt\ell}$ and $$\sum_{\{a,u,v\}\text{ a triangle of }F'}q_aq_uq_v \ge \left(1-C_2\frac{\log\ell}{\sqrt\ell}\right) \sum_{\{a,u,v\}\text{ a triangle of }F}q_aq_uq_v, \label{eq:split-retention}$$ where $C_2$ depends only on $r$. The projection $F'\to F$ is a homomorphism injective on directed edges.
  - Explanation: Vertex splitting reduces neighborhood weight while retaining a controlled fraction of weighted triangles.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-walk-entropy-bound)): With the above definitions, $$H_0\ge-\log\ell,\qquad H_R\le O_r(\log\ell). \label{eq:walk-entropy-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Neighborhood-walk entropy has a logarithmic upper bound, permitting a small entropy increment.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-one-round)): For all sufficiently large $\Delta$ depending only on $r$, suppose that a proper partial correspondence coloring and entry weights satisfy availability, <a href="#eq:weight-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weight-bounds">[eq:weight-bounds]</a>, and $$\label{eq:mass-window} K/2\le W_v\le2K\qquad(v\in V(G')).$$ There is an extension of the partial coloring and a choice of weights on the remaining entries that preserve availability and <a href="#eq:weight-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weight-bounds">[eq:weight-bounds]</a>, and satisfy $$\label{eq:round-conclusions} W_v^{\mathrm{new}}\le W_v+\xi,\qquad F_v^{\mathrm{new}}\le F_v+\xi,\qquad \frac{\deg_{\mathrm{new}}(v)}{\Delta} \le(1-\tau)\frac{\deg_{G'}(v)}{\Delta}+\xi$$ for every vertex $v$ that remains uncolored.
  - Explanation: A coloring round extends the partial coloring, controls weight and potential, and reduces the remaining degree.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-round-expectation)): For the random update just defined, $$\label{eq:round-expectations} \begin{aligned} \mathbb E\bar W_v&\le W_v+o(\xi),\\ \mathbb E\bar F_v&\le F_v+\tau(B+1)D_v+o(\xi),\\ \mathbb E\bar D_{vu}&\ge\tfrac12D_{vu}\qquad(vu\in E(G')). \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Expected provisional weights and potentials satisfy the stated error bounds, and matching energy retains a fixed fraction in expectation.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-read-concentration)): Let $X_1,\ldots,X_m$ be bounded real functions of finitely many independent inputs. Suppose that $X_j$ has range of length at most $b_j$, and that each input is used by at most $p$ of the functions, where $p\ge1$ is real. If $\sum_jb_j^2>0$, then for every $a>0$, $$\label{eq:input-concentration} \mathbb P\left(\sum_j(X_j-\mathbb EX_j)\ge a\right),\quad \mathbb P\left(\sum_j(X_j-\mathbb EX_j)\le-a\right) \ \le\ \exp\left(-\frac{2a^2}{p\sum_jb_j^2}\right).$$ If $\sum_jb_j^2=0$, both probabilities are zero.
  - Explanation: Bounded participation of independent inputs yields exponential upper and lower tail bounds for a sum of bounded functions.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-activation-collisions)): Let $\mathcal E_v$ be the set of edges of $H$ with an endpoint hosted at a neighbor of $v$ in $G'$, and let $X_v$ count the edges of $\mathcal E_v$ whose two endpoints activate. Then $$\label{eq:collision-tail} \mathbb P\bigl(4K\Delta^{-0.99}X_v>\zeta\bigr) \le O(\Delta^3)e^{e-1-\Delta^{0.01}}+e^{-\Delta^{0.8}}.$$ For either coefficient family in <a href="#eq:neighbor-coefficients" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:neighbor-coefficients">[eq:neighbor-coefficients]</a>, the sum of $b_u$ over hosts $u$ counted by $A_u$ that receive no color is at most $4K\Delta^{-0.99}X_v$.
  - Explanation: Conflicting activations have a small tail probability and bound the weight of activated hosts that receive no color.

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-adjustment",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $r\\ge4$ there are constants $A=A(r)>0$, $B=B(r)\\ge1$, and $\\Lambda_r\\ge1$ with the following property. Let $\\Lambda\\ge\\Lambda_r$, and let $H$ be a finite $K_r$-free graph whose vertex set has a partition $\\mathcal D$ such that each vertex has at most one neighbor in each part. For any positive vertex weights with $s_i\\le\\Lambda$, there is a finite family of the multiplier and drop operations defined above, with vectors $g^{(j)}$, listed supports $J_j$, and rates $a_j\\ge0$, such that $$\\label{eq:adjustment}\n t_i+\\sum_j a_jg_i^{(j)}\\le B d_i,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{j:\\,i\\in J_j}a_j\\le\\Lambda^4\n \\qquad(i\\in V(H)).$$ The constants depend only on $r$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sparse-multipliers",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $r\\ge2$ there is a constant $D_r\\ge1$ such that the following holds. Let $F$ be a finite $K_r$-free graph with positive vertex weights $q_i$ of total mass $s$. For every $0<\\epsilon\\le1/2$, there are jointly distributed random multipliers $n_i$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:sparse-multipliers}\n 0\\le n_i\\le D_r\\epsilon^{-(r-2)},\\qquad\n \\mathbb En_i=1,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{iu\\in E(F)}q_iq_u n_i n_u\\le\\epsilon s^2,$$ where the edge sum is unordered.",

        "exact_quote": "Assume the prices satisfy <a href=\"#eq:dual-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dual-test\">[eq:dual-test]</a>, and fix a level $b$. Suppose that $$32\\le h\\le20\\ell_0^2,\\qquad q(S),q(D)\\le e^h,\n \\qquad 0\\le\\eta\\le2,$$ and that $\\alpha_i,\\beta_i\\le\\eta$ for $i\\in S$.         "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a graph with a homomorphism $p:F\\to H_b$ that is injective on directed edges. Give every vertex of $F$ the weight, prices, coefficients, and part of its image.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-triangle-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "Let $32\\le\\ell\\le\\ell_0$, and let $F$ be a nonempty finite graph with no isolated vertices, with a homomorphism into some $H_b$ injective on directed edges. Give its vertices the inherited weights, prices, and parts, and suppose that <a href=\"#eq:prepared-level\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:prepared-level\">[eq:prepared-level]</a> holds. There is a graph $F'$ obtained by splitting vertices and retaining at most one copy of each edge of $F$, with unchanged vertex weights, such that every neighborhood of $F'$ has weight at most $e^{\\sqrt\\ell}$ and $$\\sum_{\\{a,u,v\\}\\text{ a triangle of }F'}q_aq_uq_v\n \\ge \\left(1-C_2\\frac{\\log\\ell}{\\sqrt\\ell}\\right)\n       \\sum_{\\{a,u,v\\}\\text{ a triangle of }F}q_aq_uq_v,\n \\label{eq:split-retention}$$ where $C_2$ depends only on $r$. The projection $F'\\to F$ is a homomorphism injective on directed edges.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Correspondence assignment** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *correspondence assignment* on a graph $G$ consists of pairwise disjoint finite sets $L(v)$, one for each vertex, and a matching $M_{uv}\subseteq L(u)\times L(v)$ for each edge $uv$.
- **Coloring of an assignment** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A coloring of the assignment chooses $c(v)\in L(v)$ at every vertex so that $(c(u),c(v))\notin M_{uv}$ for every edge $uv$.
- **Correspondence chromatic number** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The *correspondence chromatic number*, also called the *DP-chromatic number*, $\chi_{\mathrm{DP}}(G)$, is the least integer $k$ for which every correspondence assignment with $\|L(v)\|\ge k$ admits a coloring.
- **Forbidden ordinary subgraph** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a fixed finite graph $F$, call $G$ *$F$-free* if it contains no subgraph isomorphic to $F$; the subgraph need not be induced.
- **Directed-edge-injective homomorphism** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A graph homomorphism is *injective on directed edges* if distinct ordered adjacent pairs have distinct images.
- **Multiplier operation** ([source](#eq:multiplier-vector)): A *multiplier operation* consists of a nonempty listed support $J\subseteq V(H)$, contained in a ball of radius $R_0$ in $H$ and satisfying $\|J\cap D\|\le P_0$ for every $D\in\mathcal D$, together with multipliers $m_i\in[0,U]$ for $i\in J$.
- **Available entry** ([source](#sec:rounds)): An entry at an uncolored vertex is *available* if it conflicts with none of the colors already chosen at neighboring vertices.
- **Relative entropy with respect to vertex weights** ([source](#subsec:neighborhood-entropy)): We therefore measure entropy relative to $q$: for a probability row $p$ on a finite set with positive weights $q$, put $$\mathcal H_q(p)=\sum_{z:p(z)>0}p(z)\log\frac{q_z}{p(z)}.$$

      "exact_definition": "A *correspondence assignment* on a graph $G$ consists of pairwise disjoint finite sets $L(v)$, one for each vertex, and a matching $M_{uv}\\subseteq L(u)\\times L(v)$ for each edge $uv$.",
      "interpretation": "Each edge specifies a matching of forbidden choices between its endpoint lists.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Correspondence assignment",
      "scope": "Finite simple undirected graphs and their correspondence assignments."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:introduction",

          "anchor": "sec:introduction",
          "exact_quote": "A coloring of the assignment chooses $c(v)\\in L(v)$ at every vertex so that $(c(u),c(v))\\notin M_{uv}$ for every edge $uv$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-184.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A coloring of the assignment chooses $c(v)\\in L(v)$ at every vertex so that $(c(u),c(v))\\notin M_{uv}$ for every edge $uv$.",
      "interpretation": "One entry is selected at each vertex while avoiding all forbidden pairs.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Coloring of an assignment",
      "scope": "Finite simple undirected graphs and their correspondence assignments."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:introduction",
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "sec:introduction",
          "exact_quote": "The *correspondence chromatic number*, also called the *DP-chromatic number*, $\\chi_{\\mathrm{DP}}(G)$, is the least integer $k$ for which every correspondence assignment with $|L(v)|\\ge k$ admits a coloring.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-184.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "The *correspondence chromatic number*, also called the *DP-chromatic number*, $\\chi_{\\mathrm{DP}}(G)$, is the least integer $k$ for which every correspondence assignment with $|L(v)|\\ge k$ admits a coloring.",
      "interpretation": "A universal list-size threshold over all correspondence assignments.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Correspondence chromatic number",
      "scope": "Finite simple undirected graphs and their correspondence assignments."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:introduction",

        {
          "anchor": "sec:introduction",
          "exact_quote": "For a fixed finite graph $F$, call $G$ *$F$-free* if it contains no subgraph isomorphic to $F$; the subgraph need not be induced.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-184.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For a fixed finite graph $F$, call $G$ *$F$-free* if it contains no subgraph isomorphic to $F$; the subgraph need not be induced.",
      "interpretation": "The exclusion concerns arbitrary subgraphs rather than only induced subgraphs.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Forbidden ordinary subgraph",

    },
    {

          "exact_quote": "A graph homomorphism is *injective on directed edges* if distinct ordered adjacent pairs have distinct images.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-184.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A graph homomorphism is *injective on directed edges* if distinct ordered adjacent pairs have distinct images.",
      "interpretation": "Distinct ordered adjacent pairs retain distinct images under projection.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Directed-edge-injective homomorphism",
      "scope": "Auxiliary graph projections used in splitting."

          "exact_quote": "A *multiplier operation* consists of a nonempty listed support $J\\subseteq V(H)$, contained in a ball of radius $R_0$ in $H$ and satisfying $|J\\cap D|\\le P_0$ for every $D\\in\\mathcal D$, together with multipliers $m_i\\in[0,U]$ for $i\\in J$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-184.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A *multiplier operation* consists of a nonempty listed support $J\\subseteq V(H)$, contained in a ball of radius $R_0$ in $H$ and satisfying $|J\\cap D|\\le P_0$ for every $D\\in\\mathcal D$, together with multipliers $m_i\\in[0,U]$ for $i\\in J$.",
      "interpretation": "The listed support satisfies a graph-radius constraint and a per-part cardinality constraint.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Multiplier operation",
      "scope": "Partitioned positively weighted clique-free graphs."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:rounds",

          "anchor": "sec:rounds",
          "exact_quote": "An entry at an uncolored vertex is *available* if it conflicts with none of the colors already chosen at neighboring vertices.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-184.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "An entry at an uncolored vertex is *available* if it conflicts with none of the colors already chosen at neighboring vertices.",
      "interpretation": "Positive-weight entries must avoid all colors already selected at neighboring hosts.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Available entry",

    },

          "exact_quote": "We therefore measure entropy relative to $q$: for a probability row $p$ on a finite set with positive weights $q$, put $$\\mathcal H_q(p)=\\sum_{z:p(z)>0}p(z)\\log\\frac{q_z}{p(z)}.$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-184.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "We therefore measure entropy relative to $q$: for a probability row $p$ on a finite set with positive weights $q$, put $$\\mathcal H_q(p)=\\sum_{z:p(z)>0}p(z)\\log\\frac{q_z}{p(z)}.$$",
      "interpretation": "The entropy measures a probability row relative to positive vertex weights rather than counting its support.",

| Q1 | \chi_{\mathrm{DP}}(G) | ≤ | $\left\lceil C_r\frac{\Delta}{\log\Delta}\right\rceil$ | The clique is fixed and the maximum degree is sufficiently large. |
| Q2 | \chi_{\mathrm{DP}}(G) | ≤ | $\left\lceil C_F\frac{\Delta}{\log\Delta}\right\rceil$ | The forbidden ordinary subgraph is fixed and the maximum degree is sufficiently large. |
| Q3 | K | = | $16(B+2)$ | Fixed clique parameter; sufficiently large original maximum degree. |
| Q4 | \gamma | = | $0.003$ | Fixed clique parameter; sufficiently large original maximum degree. |
| Q5 | C | = | $\frac{100K}{\gamma}$ | Fixed clique parameter; sufficiently large original maximum degree. |
| Q6 | k | = | $\left\lceil\frac{C\Delta}{\log\Delta}\right\rceil$ | Fixed clique parameter; sufficiently large original maximum degree. |
| Q7 | w_* | = | $\frac Kk$ | Parameters for sufficiently large original maximum degree. |
| Q8 | \Lambda | = | $20\log\Delta$ | Parameters for sufficiently large original maximum degree. |
| Q9 | \tau | = | $\Delta^{-0.10}$ | Parameters for sufficiently large original maximum degree. |
| Q10 | \xi | = | $\Delta^{-0.14}$ | Parameters for sufficiently large original maximum degree. |
| Q11 | \zeta | = | $\Delta^{-0.15}$ | Parameters for sufficiently large original maximum degree. |
| Q12 | T_* | = | $\left\lceil2\tau^{-1}\log\Delta\right\rceil$ | A finite upper limit on the number of coloring rounds. |
| Q13 | \deg_{\mathrm{remaining}}(v)/\Delta | ≤ | $\Delta^{-2}+\Delta^{-0.04}$ | Remaining vertices after the prescribed rounds. |
| Q14 | \bigl\|\{i\in L(v):w_i>0\}\bigr\| | ≥ | $\frac K2\Delta^{0.99}$ | Positive-weight available entries after the prescribed rounds. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-sparse-multipliers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sparse-multipliers): Induction through clique-free neighborhoods with random selection of disjoint removed sets. -> Mean-one bounded sparse multipliers.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-cross) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-cross): Test the normalized dual inequality using sparse multipliers and deletions. -> A directed local cross-edge estimate.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-triangle-splitting) / [proof](#subsec:coupled-splitting): Entropy increments and simultaneous coupling produce qualified incidence labels. -> A split graph with reduced neighborhood weight and controlled triangle loss.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-triangle-bound) / [proof](#subsec:triangle-iteration): Repeated splitting and controlled deletion losses reduce neighborhood weights. -> A uniform triangle-to-edge mass bound.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-adjustment) / [proof](#pm-h-10): Closed-cone separation turns the scalar dual estimate into simultaneous local operations. -> A finite family of local weight-adjustment operations and rates.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-one-round) / [proof](#pm-h-21): Independent flags, bounded-participation concentration, local-lemma selection, and simultaneous trimming. -> A round preserving the weight conditions and controlling degree, mass, and potential.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:completion): Iterate the round and then color remaining vertices greedily. -> A proper correspondence coloring.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **assignment**: Choices are taken from disjoint finite lists and conflicts on an edge form a matching.
- **completion**: Greedy completion has a choice at every step when the number of available entries exceeds the remaining maximum degree.
- **asymptotic**: The correspondence coloring estimate requires a fixed excluded clique and sufficiently large maximum degree.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **arbitrary-conflicts**: Greedily resolve arbitrary pairwise forbidden choices. -> A neighbor can forbid many available entries when its forbidden pairs do not form a matching.
- **effective-threshold**: Treat the asymptotic estimate as a numerical list-size prescription for an arbitrary finite instance. -> The degree threshold and coloring constant are not numerically specified.
- **round-algorithm**: Treat greedy completion as an implementation of the whole coloring proof. -> The completion procedure begins only after the rounds have produced sufficiently many available entries.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The final greedy completion is a finite constructive step once available entries outnumber the remaining maximum degree. This panel implements that step only; it does not compute the unspecified asymptotic constants or generate the preceding coloring rounds.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON batch of finite correspondence assignments with integer entry identifiers.
# PRECONDITIONS: Simple undirected graph; disjoint nonempty lists; edge constraints are matchings; each list exceeds maximum degree.
# OUTPUTS: One proper entry choice per vertex, or REJECT for a violated precondition.
# TIME: Polynomial in explicit graph, lists, and matching input size; sequential greedy scans.
# SPACE: Linear in explicit input size and stored choices.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers, including python-flint fmpz degree comparisons.
# FAILURE: Invalid assignments return REJECT; malformed JSON fails explicitly.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz

def complete(x):
    try:
        lists, edges = x['lists'], x['edges']
        n = len(lists)
        if any(not L or any(type(c) is not int for c in L) for L in lists):
            return 'REJECT'
        flat = [c for L in lists for c in L]
        if len(flat) != len(set(flat)):
            return 'REJECT'
        degree = [fmpz(0) for _ in lists]
        seen = set()
        adj = [[] for _ in lists]
        for u, v, pairs in edges:
            if type(u) is not int or type(v) is not int or not 0 <= u < v < n or (u,v) in seen:
                return 'REJECT'
            seen.add((u,v))
            degree[u] += 1
            degree[v] += 1
            if any(a not in lists[u] or b not in lists[v] for a,b in pairs):
                return 'REJECT'
            if len({a for a,b in pairs}) != len(pairs) or len({b for a,b in pairs}) != len(pairs):
                return 'REJECT'
            adj[u].append((v, pairs))
            adj[v].append((u, [(b,a) for a,b in pairs]))
        cap = max(degree, default=fmpz(0))
        if any(fmpz(len(L)) <= cap for L in lists):
            return 'REJECT'
        chosen = []
        for v, L in enumerate(lists):
            blocked = {a for u,pairs in adj[v] if u < v for a,b in pairs if chosen[u] == b}
            c = next((a for a in L if a not in blocked), None)
            if c is None:
                raise RuntimeError('greedy precondition invariant failed')
            chosen.append(c)
        return chosen
    except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
        return 'REJECT'

print(json.dumps([complete(x) for x in json.load(sys.stdin)]))
```

    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON batch of finite correspondence assignments with integer entry identifiers.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Simple undirected graph; disjoint nonempty lists; edge constraints are matchings; each list exceeds maximum degree.\n# OUTPUTS: One proper entry choice per vertex, or REJECT for a violated precondition.\n# TIME: Polynomial in explicit graph, lists, and matching input size; sequential greedy scans.\n# SPACE: Linear in explicit input size and stored choices.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers, including python-flint fmpz degree comparisons.\n# FAILURE: Invalid assignments return REJECT; malformed JSON fails explicitly.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpz\n\ndef complete(x):\n    try:\n        lists, edges = x['lists'], x['edges']\n        n = len(lists)\n        if any(not L or any(type(c) is not int for c in L) for L in lists):\n            return 'REJECT'\n        flat = [c for L in lists for c in L]\n        if len(flat) != len(set(flat)):\n            return 'REJECT'\n        degree = [fmpz(0) for _ in lists]\n        seen = set()\n        adj = [[] for _ in lists]\n        for u, v, pairs in edges:\n            if type(u) is not int or type(v) is not int or not 0 <= u < v < n or (u,v) in seen:\n                return 'REJECT'\n            seen.add((u,v))\n            degree[u] += 1\n            degree[v] += 1\n            if any(a not in lists[u] or b not in lists[v] for a,b in pairs):\n                return 'REJECT'\n            if len({a for a,b in pairs}) != len(pairs) or len({b for a,b in pairs}) != len(pairs):\n                return 'REJECT'\n            adj[u].append((v, pairs))\n            adj[v].append((u, [(b,a) for a,b in pairs]))\n        cap = max(degree, default=fmpz(0))\n        if any(fmpz(len(L)) <= cap for L in lists):\n            return 'REJECT'\n        chosen = []\n        for v, L in enumerate(lists):\n            blocked = {a for u,pairs in adj[v] if u < v for a,b in pairs if chosen[u] == b}\n            c = next((a for a in L if a not in blocked), None)\n            if c is None:\n                raise RuntimeError('greedy precondition invariant failed')\n            chosen.append(c)\n        return chosen\n    except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):\n        return 'REJECT'\n\nprint(json.dumps([complete(x) for x in json.load(sys.stdin)]))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A static software assignment problem can inherit the coloring existence bound if tasks and choices admit the stated graph and edge-matching model. This is a conditional modeling transfer; no software deployment is supplied. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A game rule, simulator, or update semantics is ABSENT. The finite graph coloring result alone does not establish an application to game state evolution. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A conflict-assignment checking tool can use the source definition as a correctness contract for a proposed coloring. The stronger list-size guarantee remains conditional on the theorem hypotheses. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The round construction and greedy completion provide an algorithmic blueprint for correspondence coloring. Selection of adjustment operations and simultaneous successful rounds must be made effective before claiming an executable logarithmic coloring procedure. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper uses partitioned graphs and weighted entries, but a new representation, update interface, or data-structure performance guarantee is ABSENT. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An accelerator architecture, arithmetic implementation, or hardware performance contract is ABSENT. Finite graph operations alone do not establish accelerator applicability. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A security primitive, adversarial model, or cryptographic guarantee is ABSENT. Avoiding forbidden color pairs supplies a combinatorial correctness condition only. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The entropy term controls a coloring potential. A learned model, training objective, or statistical generalization result is ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Finite allocation problems with pairwise matching incompatibilities can use the coloring bound as a conditional feasibility guarantee. The closed-cone argument supplies an existence certificate for local adjustments rather than a prescribed optimizer. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Local supports and bounded participation are proof conditions. A distributed communication model, message bound, or decentralized method for selecting simultaneous successful rounds is ABSENT. |
| other (graph_theory_and_combinatorics) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The stated mathematical application is correspondence coloring of clique-free graphs, with list and ordinary coloring as special cases and a reduction for a fixed excluded ordinary subgraph. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-graph-model** (hypothesis): The result concerns finite simple undirected graphs and correspondence assignments whose forbidden pairs on each edge form a matching. Blocked use: ['Unmodeled directed, infinite, or non-pairwise constraints.', 'Arbitrary forbidden-pair relations that fail the matching condition.']
- **L-asymptotic** (hypothesis): The logarithmic coloring bound requires a fixed excluded clique and sufficiently large maximum degree. The constants and degree threshold depend on the fixed clique parameter. Blocked use: ['Using the logarithmic guarantee below its degree threshold.', 'Allowing the excluded clique parameter to vary while treating the bound as uniform.']
- **L-effective-constants** (unknown): Explicit numerical values for the main bound constant and its sufficient degree threshold are ABSENT. The paper does not optimize either. Blocked use: ['Treating the asymptotic bound as a numerically calibrated capacity estimate.']
- **L-subgraph** (hypothesis): The forbidden graph is excluded as an ordinary subgraph; induced-subgraph exclusion alone does not establish the corollary hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Replacing ordinary-subgraph exclusion by induced-subgraph exclusion.']
- **L-universal** (formal_scope): The correspondence chromatic number guarantees colorability for every assignment at the given list-size threshold. A successful coloring of a single assignment does not establish that universal guarantee. Blocked use: ['Certifying the correspondence chromatic number from a single successful assignment.']
- **L-existential-rounds** (nonconstructive): The simultaneous round argument selects a trial through an existence argument. An effective selection procedure and a runtime bound for the full logarithmic coloring construction are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Presenting the full asymptotic existence proof as a certified executable coloring algorithm.']
- **L-participation** (hypothesis): Small support radius alone does not give the concentration needed for correspondence coloring; participation within each list must also be bounded. Blocked use: ['Replacing the per-list support bound by a radius bound alone.']
- **L-greedy-scope** (formal_scope): Greedy completion requires available entries that outnumber the remaining maximum degree, together with the matching condition. This final subroutine does not establish that the preceding rounds produce its input. Blocked use: ['Claiming greedy completion alone achieves the logarithmic bound.', 'Using the completion step without sufficient available entries.']
- **L-weight-state** (hypothesis): The coloring round requires availability, the specified weight bounds, and a total-weight window; arbitrary weighted states are outside that proposition. Blocked use: ['Applying the round guarantee to weights that fail its maintained assumptions.']
- **L-split-map** (hypothesis): Transfer of the local cross estimate through splitting requires a projection injective on directed edges and the stated mass, coefficient, radius, and participation conditions. Blocked use: ['Transferring the local estimate along an arbitrary graph homomorphism.']
- **L-empty** (edge_case): An empty auxiliary graph is allowed, and the adjustment lemma then holds with no operations. Blocked use: ['Requiring a nonempty operation family for the empty auxiliary graph.']
- **L-natural-log** (hypothesis): All logarithms are natural; numerical use of the stated constants must preserve that convention. Blocked use: ['Changing logarithm convention while retaining the same numerical constants.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every fixed integer <i>r</i> ≥ 4, we prove that every <i>K</i><sub><i>r</i></sub>-free graph of sufficiently large maximum degree Δ has correspondence chromatic number $`O_r(\Delta/\log\Delta)`$. This resolves the Alon–Krivelevich–Sudakov coloring conjecture in the stronger correspondence-coloring form. The same bound, with a constant depending on <i>F</i>, holds when any fixed graph <i>F</i> is excluded as an ordinary subgraph. Ordinary and list coloring satisfy the same bounds.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index
